[T&A 8Dec09] Not sure if there is a problem here, but “Egpakariyu-diyu” seems an unnatural translation of “he departed/left” and sounds like it is a separate action from “imbatun e”. Actually these are the same action. His rising up into heaven was the same as his departing from them. He departed from them, and more specifically he departed from them by rising up to heaven. Normally we use words like “awe” or “engked” or “gaat” for an action like this. Suggest: “He left them behind as he was taken_up to heaven.” [JLM 2Feb10] Actually the meaning here is the process of departing slowly from them. As he say his blessing, he was simultaneously leaving them then totally gone. He didn’t walk away. He went away from them by rising up into the air. Also, I think you need to return the “diye te langit” to the end of the sentence for clarity and accuracy.
